755. JOSEPH PRIESTLY was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of February, 24 pennies and 36 halfpennies, the monies of Sarah Gurney, his mistress.
SARAH GURNEY. I am a widow, and keep the Old King's Arms, Gray's Inn-lane. On the 27th of January I sent the prisoner, with 3s. 6d. in copper, to Mr. Bennett; he returned, and I asked if he had paid the money - he said Yes; I afterwards saw Bennett, and in consequence of what he said I spoke to the prisoner - he said he had not paid the money, that he had got into bad company and lost it - he ran away directly; he had been eleven months with me, and had been a good boy for about nine months.
HENRY BENNETT. I live in Upper North-place, Gray's Inn-lane. On the 27th of January I served the prisoner with some ribbon, on the account of Mrs. Gurney, which came to 3s. 6d. - he never came to pay me for it.
JOHN BURTON. I was an officer. I took the prisoner on the 3rd of February; he begged I would not take him, and in going along he said his clothes and handkerchief, which were in his box, would settle it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320216-248]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
756. ISABELLA DONOVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 1 cap, value 3s. 6d., the goods of Joseph Allen.
ANN LOUISA ALLEN. I am the wife of Joseph Allen. I keep a milliner's shop in Norton-falgate; the prisoner came there on the 17th of January, alone - I was standing in the back parlour; I saw her take up a cap out of the window, and a blue handkerchief - I went into the shop, and when I saw her I knew her very well, and thought it was of no use to speak to her then - she dropped the cap, as I suppose, between herself and the counter; I went into the parlour again, and told my girls to watch - they gave me information; I went out when they said, "Now, Ma'am;" I found her with a cap, which she had taken - he came to buy nothing, but in the same manner as she had come before - she desired me to put a cap by for her, and not to mix it.
CAROLINE LITTLE. I was in the parlour - Mrs. Allen came into the parlour and told us to watch, and I saw the prisoner drop the cap; then she stopped down and put something into her bosom.
HENRY DEPIES. I am an officer, and have the cap, which I saw Mrs. Allen take from her; I heard her say,"You have robbed me, and I know you have the property;" she took this cap from her bosom - she was searched in my presence, and another new cap was found on her, and half a yard of lace; she had a sovereign and three duplicates.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I bought it about a fortnight before, of a young lady who was in her shop, for half a crown; I am a mantua-maker - there are plenty of caps like that.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-249]
757. WILLIAM BERRY, JOHN BERRY, WILLIAM TAYLOR, and BENJAMIN CATLING were indicted for stealing, on the 6th of February, 16 bushels of wheat, value 5l., the goods of William Boards.
MR. CLARKSON conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM BOARDS. I live at Edmonton, and am a farmer. John Wheeler fastened my barn on the night of the 6th of February, and next morning he told me it was broken open - I examined, and found part of the roof had been untiled, sufficient for two to have got in; I missed some wheat from the bulk - I thought about two quarters; I found a hat in the barn, which I believe is here - I gave notice to Camp, the officer, and went with him to Taylor's, at a place called Journeyman's-green, on the following day - I think Wheeler went with me; Catling lives next door to Taylor - I found at Taylor's a bed-tick filled with wheat in the chaff, and the bed clothes on it - the bed appeared made; I think there were about six bushels in the tick, and I found about five pecks in a sack in the house -I have compared the wheat I found there with that in my barn, and I believe it to be part of the same wheat; I found at Catling's about five pecks of wheat, from which the chaff had been blown, and some chaff was buried by the side of the house.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Do you pretend to distinguish your wheat from other wheat? A. As far as possible, I think I can - there is much difference in thequality, weight, and colour of it; it was in the chaff, in which state it is not sold - it was very good, and cleaner than it is in general; that I found at Catling's was not in the chaff, but it was not then in a marketable state - it was in such a state as a poor person might have it for his own use; Catling was in my service about three years ago - I do not know that we had any difference; I do not recollect having a fowl injured or killed, or talking of instituting a prosecution against any body, and applying to Catling to give evidence - he made a false charge against me, but I am ready to meet it in any way.
JOHN WHEELER. I am thresher to Mr. Boards. I made his barn fast on the night of the 6th of February, and the next morning I found the roof broken - I think about two quarters of wheat were missing; I saw the wheat which was found at the prisoner's; in my judgment it was the same as the bulk - that found at Catling's had been partly cleaned, but not entirely.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you not said before that you could not say that it was the same wheat? A. No, Sir, I never said so to any one - I said I could not positively swear to it, but I believe it was the wheat.
DANIEL BARNES. I am a watchman of Edmonton. On the night of the 6th of February, at twenty minutes past ten o'clock I saw the four prisoners together, at the William the Fourth beer-shop, which is about three quarters of a mile from Mr. Boards'; (they had gone through the town about nine) - as I was proceeding on my beat they overtook me in a lane which leads to Mr. Boards' barn, but about half a mile from it - this was about halfpast ten o'clock; Taylor and Catling collared me, and said they did not care for such a d-d watchman as me; I had not done any thing to them.
Cross-examined. Q. Were they in the road to Catling's house? A. Yes, they could go that way; I should not have noticed them if they had not noticed me.
JAMES LAMBERT. I am a shoe-maker, and live at Edmonton. Camp came to me on the 7th of February - I told him where the two Berrys lived; I have seen this hat which was found in the barn; it is William Berry 's hat; I have often seen him wear it, and John Berry also - I went with Camp to the barn and saw some footsteps in a track, which corresponded with the shoes of William and John Berry - I measured the width of them; I have compared them with this shoe, which William Berry had on - here is a hob on one side of their shoes, which had made a mark in the footsteps - I had sold them the shoes; I know they are theirs.
JOHN CAMP. I am a constable. I went with Mr. Boards to Taylor and Catling's - I have been a farmer, and in my judgment the wheat found corresponds with the bulk - I know that Lambert got the shoes from Berry; when I took the Berrys, they said they were at home by nine o'clock, and then they said it was nothing to me - there is a communication from Catling's house to Taylor's, by a trap-door; here is the hat which was found in the barn.
Cross-examined. Q. Are they not both under the same roof? A. Yes, it is not common for houses of that description to have a communication - I never saw it before; the partition-wall is usually carried up to the top; the door is in the partition and pulls up - it is in the up stairs room; you go under the roof which hangs over, in a sloping way - the wheat found at Catling's, has some chaff in it now - Catling and Taylor were not at home when I went; Catling gave himself up the next morning.
THOMAS GILES. I went with Camp; what he has stated is correct.
William Berry 's Defence. When the constable came to me, he asked where my hat was, and I showed him the one I have now in my hand; it is the one I have always worn when I have carried a fish basket; but I have, for the last three weeks, been working on the road; Lambert is my brother-in-law - he rushed into my house three months before and cut my neck with a knife; he has used my sister very ill - he has said he would do me an injury, and hang me if he could.
John Berry 's Defence. I never saw that hat in my life - I know nothing about it.
Taylor's Defence. The wheat found in my house was only a bushel and a half.
Catling's Defence. The wheat found at my house 'I had from my sister Hannah Fuller, and I thought it would be more good to me when my wife was confined than before.
HANNAH FULLER. I am Catling's sister. His wife, my brother, and I, gleaned some wheat, and Catling had it about a week before from my house in Silver-street.
MR. CLARKSON. Q. When did you glean it? A. In August last, about Edmonton - we gleaned sometimes in three or four fields in a day; this was mixed wheat - we gleaned some of Mr. Boards' and some of Mr. Knight's.
COURT. Q. Did you permit the small wheat to remain with it? A. We cleaned it ourselves in a little sieve on a sheet - I put the chaff on a little piece of ground before my garden; I know my sister had two chaff pillows given to her.
JURY to MR. BOARDS. Q. Did the chaff you found buried appear to be fresh buried? A. It was not matted as chaff usually is, which has been in a pillow or a bed.
W. BERRY - GUILTY. Aged 33.
J. BERRY - GUILTY. Aged 32.
TAYLOR - GUILTY. Aged 32.
CATLING - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-250]
758. MICHAEL HOPKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of January, 1 cap, value 3s., the goods of John William Willis.
RICHARD CHIPPERFIELD. I live with Mr. John William Willis, a hatter. I was in his glass-case cleaning his window on the 10th of January - I saw the prisoner step in, take a cap, and run off; I overtook him in Bowlyard, St. Giles' - he had parted with the cap in Charles-street - it has not been found.
GUILTY. Aged 13. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-251]
759. GEORGE NASH and JAMES LAW were indicted for stealing, on the 19th of January, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Hawkins, from his person.
THOMAS HAWKINS. I was in the Strand about six o'clock in the evening, on the 19th of January: I had a handkerchief in my pocket - I turned, and saw it on the pavement; the prisoners were in custody.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. You did notsee them till they were in custody? A. No; they were then within a yard of me; there were other persons in the street - there was a female at some distance.
THOMAS TIPPER. I am a Policeman. On Thursday, the 19th of January, I was in the Strand, and saw the two prisoners following the prosecutor - I saw Nash put his right hand into his pocket, draw the handkerchief out, and give it to Law - I secured them both; Law threw it down; there was another with them, who escaped.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you known Nash before? A. I have seen him with others.
The prisoners received a good character.
NASH - GUILTY. Aged 18.
LAW - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-252]
760. HENRY FITZGERALD was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of January, 3 leather-skins, value 6s.; 17 trunk-plates, value 1s., and 1 lock and key, value 6d., the goods of Thomas Maltwood, his master.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS MALTWOOD. I am a trunk-maker, and live in Oxford-street - the prisoner was my apprentice; he has served four years of his time - Robert Barker was a journeyman of mine formerly. He called on me, and gave me some information - he brought me three brown sheepskins, a lock, and seventeen iron trunk plates; it was on a Sunday morning that Barker brought them, and on the Tuesday morning the prisoner called me aside when I came up from breakfast - he began the conversation himself; he said, "I took three skins and the lock to Mr. Barker to make a trunk, for which I had an order, but I have not got the order, and I will fetch them;" I told him it was then too late, Barker had told me all about it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is it not the custom, if young men want skins for business, to take them without asking you? A. No, never - I know Mr. White; I have granted him the materials to make a trunk for a friend when he asked me, but my men never take skins without asking me - I had discharged Barker, not having employment for him.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Do you ever permit your apprentices to have any work of their own? A. No; I always strictly forbid it - I have kept Barker employed out of doors doing what I could send him.
ROBERT BARKER. I am a trunk-maker - I did live at Mr. Maltwood's; I live in Greece-street. The prisoner came to me on Saturday, the 28th of January; he said he wanted a trunk for a friend, and asked if I would make it for him - he said I should find the wood work, and he would buy the rest of the materials; I said I would make it for about 16s. - he said he could not give more than 12s., as he was only to have 24s. for it; this was agreed upon, and on the Sunday morning I received the leather, the lock, and the plates from my landlord's two children - as soon as I received them I went to his master, and told him- on the Tuesday morning the prisoner came to me again, and said he had not got the order for the trunk, and asked me to let him have the lock and the plates back again - he did not ask for the skins then; I said, "Where did you get these skins from?" he said, "From Mr. Maltwood's"- I said, "You can't have them, for Mr. Maltwood has them;" he said, "It is all up then," and off he ran.
Cross-examined by Mr. LEE. Q. Are the children here who delivered these articles to you? A. No; I was discharged from Mr. Maltwood's house, but I work at home; I never knew of journeymen taking materials to make a trunk, if I have asked for any thing it was given to me.
JURY. Q. When did the prisoner ask you for the skins back? A. About nine o'clock on the Tuesday morning, and he told Mr. Maltwood about ten.
EDWARD BARKER. I am an officer. I took the prisoner.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 19
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18320216-253]
761. WILLIAM QUINT was indicted for embezzlement.
ISAAC LEWIS. I am a baker, and live in Church-street, Bethnal-green. The prisoner had been my journeyman for about nine months; he took out bread and was to receive money, which he was to give an account of as soon as he came in.
SARAH ELLIS. I took in bread from the prosecutor to sell again. I paid the prisoner on Saturday, 17s. 6d. - he did not give me any bill or receipt.
SOPHIA SARAH BOYCE. I serve in Mr. Lewis' shop - the prisoner pays me the money when Mr. Lewis is not there; he did not pay me the money which he had from Mrs. Ellis.
GEORGE CADOGAN. I am an officer. I took the prisoner.
ALICE REYNOLDS. I deal with the prosecutor. I paid the prisoner one morning, when he brought the bread; I did not have any bill or book - I do not know whether I paid him any thing on the 14th of January; I know the last morning he came to my house I paid him for half a dozen loaves.
ISAAC LEWIS. He booked these as unpaid; he never paid it to me.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18320216-254]
762. JOSEPH BUTLER was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of January, 1 cloak, value 16s., the goods of William James Stevenson.
ROBERT COUSINS. I am shopman to Mr. William James Stevenson, of Ratcliff-highway. This cloak was lost from his shop, but I did not see it taken; I heard Stop thief! called, went to the door, and missed it - I did not see the prisoner that night, but a few days after Brown gave me notice that he was at the window, and I took him- the cloak has not been found.
JOHN BROWN. I live close by the prosecutor's shop. -I saw the prisoner about half-past seven o'clock on the night of the 19th of January; he walked up and down the door two or three times, he then went on the step, took the cloak, and ran up Cannon-street - I cried out Stop thief! and the alarm was given, but he made his escape - this was on Thursday, between seven and eight o'clock, and on the Tuesday after I saw him again looking in at the window; I went and told Mr. Cousins the man was outside the door, and he was taken: I know the prisoner was the man that took the cloak - it was dark, but he hadpassed me three or four times, and looked at me - I saw him for five minutes.
MR. COUSINS. Brown gave a very clear description of the prisoner the same night, and it exactly corresponded with his person.
Prisoner's Defence. He said at the office that he only knew me by my coat and hat, but, having two or three hearings, he spoke more confidently of me.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-255]
763. JOHN ARUNDELL was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 1 cart, value 3l., the goods of George Martin.
GEORGE MARTIN. I am a sawyer. On the 3rd of February, about seven o'clock in the morning, I left a cart at the corner of New-street, Bishopsgate; I chained it to a scaffold-pole about four that evening, and missed it the next morning, about nine o'clock; I afterwards found it in Wentworth-street, at Spooner's, who keeps a livery-yard- I know nothing of the prisoner; I got information, and went to the prisoner's house, in Widegate-alley - he is a sweep; I asked if he had not got a cart in Wentworth-street, and he said he had - I asked how he came by it; he said he bought it on the Saturday morning, about seven o'clock - the officer then took him to the watch-house; he did not say where he bought it.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. You found that he is a sweep? A. Yes, and I believe he keeps a donkey or two - he said he bought the cart of two men on Saturday morning; I am sure he did not say Sunday morning.
COURT. Q. Did he say who they were? A. No; he said he knew two men, and he was on bail a week to find them.
JOHN SPOONER. I keep a yard. On Saturday, the 4th, I found this cart in my yard, which I had never seen before- I inquired about, but could not hear any thing of it - the prisoner afterwards came to me about it, and asked if I was not surprised at seeing that strange cart there; I said Yes - he said, "It belongs to me - I bought it this morning for 13s.;" I knew him, and was then satisfied.
Cross-examined. Q. What has been his character? A. An honest man.
THOMAS HALL. I saw the cart from five to ten minutes after seven o'clock on Saturday morning.
WILLIAM LAW. I went to the prisoner with the prosecutor - what he has stated is correct.
Prisoner's Defence. I bought it between seven and eight o'clock on Saturday morning - it was rather too large for me, and I took it to Mr. Spooner's.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320216-256]
764. FREDERICK BRIDGE was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 trunk, value 2s., the goods of William Illman.
THOMAS STANDOVER. I live opposite Mr. Illman's, in Dean-street, Soho. On the 16th of February I saw the prisoner and some others near the house - I saw the prisoner take a trunk - he put it under his arm, and ran away; Mr. Wheeler followed him - I know he is the boy.
JAMES WHEELER. I was going out at the door, and Mr. Standover said the lad had run away with a trunk from Mr. Illman's - I went and took him with it.
JAMES LUFF. I took the prisoner, and have the trunk.
WILLIAM ILLMAN. This is my property, and was in one of my windows.
GUILTY. Aged 13. - Transported for Seven Years.

767. ELLEN BLYTH and CATHERINE BARRY were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 1 bag, value 2d.; 4 shillings, 1 sixpence, and 3d. in copper, the property of William Nitingale, from his person.
WILLIAM NITINGALE. I live in Lisle-street, Leicester-square, and am an engraver. On the 12th of February I met the prisoners in Hanover-street, Long-acre, about three o'clock in the morning; they wished me to go home with them - I refused, stating that I had no money; I then felt Blyth's hand draw my bag and money out of my pocket, as I was standing opposite the house of an acquaintance, considering whether I should wake him up to let me in; I thought Blyth gave the bag and money to Barry, but I did not see her do it - I had 4s. 6d. in silver, and 3d. in copper in it - I said if they did not give me my money, I would give them in charge; two Policemen were coming by, and they took the money up from the ground - both the prisoners said they had not got it; I am sure it was Blyth's hand which took it from me.
Blyth. Q. Did you not meet me in Hanover-street? A. I was standing there, and you came up to me - I was not so sober as I am now - I did not say I had a small trifle, and give her the money.
JOHN BENNETT. I am a Policeman. I was in Belton-street, and heard Blyth abusing the prosecutor, and using very bad language - I was going up to remove them for being disorderly, and when I got near, the prosecutor called Police - I went up, and seized Barry; she threw from her hand a purse, enclosed in this handkerchief, which she afterwards denied being hers - I said, "This is the woman that had the purse," and the prosecutor, whom I did not consider at all tipsy said, "This one (meaning Blyth) took it from me" - I took both the prisoners to the station, and found on them 4s. 6d. in silver, and 3d. in copper.
Blyth. I threw it down myself. Witness. No, Barry threw it down - Blyth was tipsy, and said she took it; she used the grossest language that ever I heard come from a woman - the prisoners are old acquaintances, and lived in the same street, in St. Giles'.
Blyth. He gave me the money to go home with him, and then he called the Policeman - Barry came up by accident, hearing the piece of work, and asked what was the matter - he said he was robbed, and I threw themoney down - I never saw Barry before that night - the prosecutor was very much in liquor.
Barry's Defence. I met the prisoner and the prosecutor in Hanover-street - I stood to see what was the matter, and the Policeman came and said that I dropped the purse, which I never had in my hand.
JOHN BENNETT. I am quite sure Barry dropped the purse from her left hand.
WILLIAM NITINGALE. They were both together when they first spoke to me - I did not see what Barry did with the purse.
BLYTH - GUILTY. Aged 22.
BARRY - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-260]
768. THOMAS CATTLE was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of February, 3 deal boards, value 8s., the goods of John Kewer.
GEORGE MILLION. I am a Police-officer. On the morning of Wednesday, the 8th of February, I stopped the prisoner in Wilderness-row, carrying three deal boards; I asked where he got them - he said of Mr. King, in Henry-street, Back-road, Islington, one hour before; I took him to Bunhill-row station - I went to look for Mr. King, and no such person lived there.
Prisoner. I told him I had them of a Mr. King - he locked me in the station, and was not gone more than three quarters of an hour, and then he said he could not find him. Witness. I went to a Mr. King - he looked at the boards, and said they were not his; on the Friday I found Mr. Kewer, and he owned them.
JOHN KEWER. I am a builder, and live in St. John-street-road. The boards are mine; I had not sold them- I had seen them safe in the morning of the 7th of February, before a house of mine in Sackville-street, about three hundred yards from where the prisoner was stopped by the officer.
Prisoner. Q. How can you swear to plain deal boards? A. They have been planed for a length of time, turned upside down, and used for scaffolding - I lost twelve of them from the same stack, and five on the Wednesday evening; they are the same length as mine, and have some cement on them - I have not a doubt of them.
Prisoner's Defence. I bought them of a young man named King for 8s. - I went up the Back-road, Islington, and we had some gin; I parted with him, and then, in mistake, I went straight on, instead of turning - when I got to St. John-square the officer stopped me.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18320216-261]
769. CATHERINE COLVIN was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of February, 1 jacket, value 15s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 5s., and 1 necklace, value 10s., the goods of James McKelvey.
JAMES McKELVEY. I am a seaman, and live in Park-street, Limehouse. On the 8th of February I came from a voyage - I went to lodge at the prisoner's house, in the Match-walk; I took there two chests, a hammock, and two parrots in a cage, which I had from Sierra Leone - the next morning I went out with the prisoner's husband to look for my wife, leaving the prisoner at home; I returned to the prisoner's house on the Thursday morning I then missed my jacket, trousers, beads, and five rupees and a half in money, which I had given her to take care of for me, and one parrot, which she had sold for 25s.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How long were you at her house? A. About eight hours; I slept there, up stairs, with one of the women - some of the women seemed to be indifferent characters; I did not make the prisoner a present of one of the parrots - the prisoner did not lend me 1s. - I did not make her any present.
THOMAS SIZER. I am in the employ of Mr. Kennedy, a pawnbroker, at Shadwell. On the 8th of February the prisoner came to the shop, and I bought four rupees and a half of her; I asked whose they were - she said her husband's, who had lately come home from the South Seas, where he had been for five years; the next morning she brought this cornelian necklace - I lent her 4s. on it.
PETER PETERSON KURBO. I lodge next door to the prisoner. On the 8th of February she gave me a pair of trousers, and told me to go and get 2s. on them, to provide breakfast for a lodger of her's - I pawned them in Charles-street for 3s.; I gave her the money and the duplicate.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you been in the house? A. Not after the prosecutor was - I go on errands for one and another.
JOHN JOHNSON. I am in the employ of a pawnbroker. I took in these trousers on the 8th of February, from Kurbo.
WILLIAM WHEELER. I am an officer. On the 9th of February I received this parrot from one Crickaley - I then took the prisoner, but found nothing on her; I asked what she had done with the rupees - she said she had spent them at different public-houses for gin, and she knew nothing about the waistcoat and trousers.
JAMES McKELVEY. These trousers and necklace are mine - the jacket has not been found; I gave no one permission to make any use of them.
Prisoner's Defence. I have witnesses who were there when he gave them to me.
ANN BATH. I live at No. 2, in the Match-walk; my husband is in Greenwich-college. I saw the prosecutor in the prisoner's place about a fortnight ago; I saw him give her a parrot, some rupees, and some beads - he was very much intoxicated, and he told her that whatever she wished to have she might have, and that on the day he was paid, he would make her a handsome present; she was quite sober - she thanked him for what he gave her; he had two parrots, and I think this is the one he gave her.
COURT. Q. Did you see him make her a present of his trousers? A. No - his two boxes were brought in, but they were not locked; he did not tell her to take care of the parrot and the rupees - he gave them to her; I was not surprised at it, a gift is a gift - there was another person present; it was between seven and eight o'clock in the evening - her husband was not there at the time.
MARY PETERSON. My husband is a butcher, and lives in Middlesex-street, Whitechapel. I was at the prisoner's house this night fortnight, I think it was - I went to inquire for a Mrs. Folger, to wash for me; the prisonerasked me to sit down - the prosecutor and her husband came in together about eight o'clock; the prosecutor sat down on a chair, and said, "Who is the mistress of this house?" the prisoner said she was - the prosecutor then gave her some beads, and some silver pieces; he said he had two birds, and he would give her one and keep the other; I did not see the birds while I was there.
COURT. Q. Did not he tell her to take care of the beads and the silver pieces for him? A. No, Sir, he gave them to her when she said she was the mistress; he said,"Here is something I have got the give you," and he pulled them out and gave them to her - they were silver foreign money, and I think the beads were red, but I was in a hurry to get home.
WILLIAM WHEELER. Here are some papers which I received from Mrs. Bath; she went by the name of Collins then - she said she had them of the prisoner; the prosecutor says they must have been taken out of his chest.
JAMES McKELVEY. I gave the prisoner the rupees to take care of for me, but the beads and the papers were taken from my chest - I brought the parrots for my two children; I paid 13s. 2d. for my lodging at the prisoner's that night, and I have the receipt in my pocket.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing of the trousers and jacket - he gave me the necklace and the rupees.
GUILTY. Aged 33. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-262]
770. HONORA CASEY was indicted for embezzlement.
REUBEN SMITH. I am a milkman, and live in Smart's-buildings, St. Giles' - the prisoner was a milk carrier in my service, for seventeen or eighteen months. It was her duty to receive money from customers.
MARY ROBINS. I am servant to Mr. Fowler, in Newman-street. On the 21st of January I paid the prisoner 8s. 10 1/2d. for milk, supplied by Mr. Smith - Mr. Smith's name was to the bill, but we do not keep the bills.
EMMA MAISEY. I am servant to Mr. Sanders, of Mornington-place. On the 23rd of January I paid the prisoner 2s. 4d., on Mr. Smith's account - I did not have a bill.
MARY AYRES. I am in the service of Mr. Hendry, of Oxford-street. I paid the prisoner 1s. 8d. on the 26th of January, which I had received from my master to be paid to Mr. Smith for milk.
REUBEN SMITH. The prisoner ought to have accounted to me for the money every day when she came home - she used to bring home some coppers every day, but she has not lately paid me any bill: I believe she cannot read or write - she has received Mr. Saunders' bills for sixteen weeks and Mr. Hendry's for twenty - I asked her about Mr. Hendry's, and she said he was out of town; I was always at home, as I had been ill for some time - I used to see her every afternoon, and when I asked if these persons had paid, she said they had not; she had 9s. a week and her breakfast.
GUILTY. Aged 22
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 3 Weeks.
[trialAccount t18320216-263]
771. THOMAS DUTFIELD was indicted for embezzlement.
ANN HUDSON. I am the wife of William Hudson, who lives in Kingsland-road, and is a butcher - the prisoner was in his service for about four months; he boarded in the house, and received weekly wages. One Thursday, I think on the 9th of February, as I was sitting in the shop, he applied to me to make out Mrs. Daniels' receipt, and said she would pay the money on Friday or Saturday morning- I gave him the receipt for 2l. 19s. 3d. - he never gave me the money; he was at home till the Saturday afternoon, and then he left us - he was taken on the Sunday morning; I asked him if he remembered that I gave him the receipt for Mrs. Daniels - he said Yes: I said, "You have not given me the money;" he said, "I know I received it, but have not given it to you;" we have heard that he is married.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did he account with you or your husband? A. With me, always; I am sure he asked me for the receipt on Thursday night, and on the Sunday the Police-officer brought him to our house - I cannot swear that money he has received on a Monday has not been paid to me till the Thursday or Friday, but it ought not to be so; he behaved very well before this.
CHARLOTTE TAPLING. I am the wife of George Tapling; he keeps the Acton Arms. On Friday, the 10th of January, the prisoner called on me for orders - I ordered a shoulder of veal, which he brought me - it came to 6s. 9d., and I paid him in two half-crowns, one shilling, and a sixpence; he owed me 3d.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you any memorandum of this? A. No; there was a small ticket with the meat, but I have mislaid it.
ELIZABETH RICHARDS. I am in the service of Mrs. Daniels, of Kingsland; I paid the prisoner 2l. 19s. 3d. on the Friday - I gave him three sovereigns, and he brought 9d. change; the officer has the bill and receipt.
MRS. HUDSON. This is the bill and receipt he had of me; I asked him on Saturday morning if Mrs. Tapling had paid him, and he said No.
Cross-examined. Q. Has he many customers to go to? A. Yes; he was to bring in the money, if any body thought proper to pay him.
RICHARD HAWKES. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner to Mrs. Hudson; she told him he had not given her the money from Mrs. Daniels - he said he had received it, but had not given it to her.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18320216-264]
772. HARRIET DAY was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of February, 1 blanket, value 3s., the goods of Joseph Wise.
MARY WISE. I am the wife of Joseph Wise; he is a carpenter, and lives in New-road, Chelsea. The prisoner and a foot soldier, who passed as her husband, hired a furnished room in our house; on a Saturday, the beginning of February, in consequence of some suspicion, I examined the bed, after she had been there a week, and missed a blanket - she said she had not had it, but I found the duplicate under the bed - on the Tuesday after they took the room the soldier was ordered to Kew.
JOHN GAMMON. I am shopman to a pawnbroker. I have a blanket, pawned by the prisoner, on the 9th of February, for 2s.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I intended to get it out again when I got the money.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18320216-265]
737. THOMAS DUTTON was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of December, 1 counterpane, value 5s.; 1 bolster, value 3s.; 2 pillows, value 3s.; 2 blankets, value 6s.; 2 sheets, value 5s., and 2 flat-irons, value 1s., the goods of John Joice.
SARAH JOICE. I am the wife of John Joice - we live in Little Windmill-street, Golden-square. The prisoner occupied a back room in my husband's house - he was taken into custody on the 8th of February, on another charge; I then went into his room, and missed the articles stated.
WILLIAM GOULD. I am apprentice to a pawnbroker. I have two blankets, pawned on the 6th of December, and two irons on the 17th of December, in the name of Thompson; I cannot say by whom, but these are the duplicates.
WILLIAM ORGER. I am an officer. I found these duplicates on the prisoner's wife; the prisoner said he took the things, and pawned them, but he was in great distress, and meant to redeem them when he got employment.
THOMAS SHIRLEY. I am a pawnbroker. I have a sheet, pawned by the prisoner on the 9th of November, in the name of Thompson - this is the duplicate I gave him.
SARAH JOICE. These are my articles. The prisoner is a dyer - they had nothing of any value of their own; they might he in distress - he owes me a good deal for rent, besides what I have lent him; his wife could earn 12s. a week, but they did not make the best use of it.
Prisoner's Defence. I am a native of Liverpool, where I was in partnership in business, but through unforeseen circumstances have been brought to distress; a gentleman at Islington has promised to employ me.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Ten Days.
[trialAccount t18320216-266]
774. JOHN DRANE was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 1 box, value 3d., and 2s. 5d. in copper monies, numbered, the property of William Morrish.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to be the property of some persons unknown.
ISAAC STEVENSON. I am clerk of the boatmen's chapel, at Paddington. On the 12th of February I saw the prisoner sitting by the fire, at the lower end of the chapel, when I went in; and while we were singing the first hymn he removed, and went near the door - he then went out; I thought something was not right - I followed, and asked how he came to leave the chapel; he said, "For God's sake, forgive me, I did it for want," and he gave me this charity-box, which had stood on the mantel-piece in the chapel - Mr. Morrish is the sub-treasurer, and any money missing he would be required to account for, but we did not know what was in it till it was opened, and 2s. 5d. was then found in it; it was not fixed.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320216-267]
775. JOHN GATEHOUSE was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of January, 1 wheelbarrow, value 10s., the goods of John Chapman.
HANNAH CHAPMAN. I am the wife of John Chapman. On the 25th of January my husband sent the prisoner with this wheelbarrow, to fetch a tub from a gentleman's house - he went away, and did not return; he had been a week in our service.
JOHN ROLSTON. I apprehended the prisoner as I was returning from Clerkenwell, on Monday week; I took him by the arm, and said, "Do you hear what Mrs. Chapman charges you with?" he said, "I don't deny taking it;" he said he had sold it to a man for 5s. or 6s. - that he could not tell the man's name, nor where he lived, but it was in the straight road from Hammersmith to Brentford; I went with him, and got it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18320216-268]
776. FREDERICK GODDING was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of February, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of William Holdsworth, from his person.
WILLIAM HOLDSWOTH. I live at Kingsland-green, and am a butcher. On the 19th of February I was in Shoreditch; I met four boy s - I rather suspected them, felt my pocket, and missed my handkerchief; I saw the prisoner crossing the road - I ran, and took him; he took my handkerchief from his pocket, and threw it down in the road; I took him to the station - this is my handkerchief.
TIMOTHY RYAN. I am a Police-officer. I received the prisoner in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 13.

[trialAccount t18320216-269]
777. WILLIAM CORNSEY was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 1 cap, value 6s., the goods of Thomas Cambridge Smith, from his person.
THOMAS CAMBRIDGE SMITH. I am the son of Ann Smith - she lives in North-street, Spitalfields. On Saturday, the 4th of February, I was in the Tenter-ground, playing; the prisoner came behind me, and took my cap; I saw him run away with it - I ran to my mother, and spoke to the Policeman, who took him.
JOHN SMITH. I was with my brother in the Tenterground - I saw the prisoner snatch the cap off his head, and run away; I saw him afterwards with the Policeman.
JOHN REYNOLDS. I saw the prisoner take the cap off Smith's head.
HENRY BOLTON. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner in Spitalfields-market on the 15th, four or five hundred yards from the Tenter-ground; he was pointed out to me by Thomas Cambridge Smith - I did not find the cap.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320216-270]
778. DANIEL HOBBS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of February, 2 flat-irons, value 18d., the goods of Jane Thompson, from the person of William Thompson .
WILLIAM THOMPSON. I am nine years and three months old, and live with my mother. I was carrying these iron to the opposite side of the way, in Waterloo-street, St. Luke's - the prisoner came, snatched them out of my hand, and ran away; they belong to a neighbour, but my mother was answerable for them.
CHARLES WORLEY. I am a pawnbroker, in the employ of Mr. Cottle, of Old-street. The prisoner pawned these flat-irons with me.
JANE THOMPSON. These are the irons - I delivered them to my son to take home; I was answerable for them.
JOHN FOY. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 12. - Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18320216-271]
779. DAVID JOHN HUGHES was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of February, 1 cap, value 4s., the goods of Benjamin King, from the person of George King .
GEORGE KING. I am ten years old. I know the prisoner - I was in Bell-street, near the Key public-house, and saw the prisoner - he came behind me, took my cap off my head, and ran away; I live in Bury-place, with my father - we sell milk; the prisoner put my cap under his coat, and ran off with it - I gave an alarm, and he was pursued.
THOMAS OLDACRE. I am butcher. I heard the alarm of Stop thief! I went into Chapple-street - I saw several persons running; the prisoner was stopped at the corner of Market-street, and I took the cap from under his coat -I am a constable of the parish; I saw the lad crying, and he said the prisoner had taken his cap - the prisoner asked me to let him go, and said he did not take it.
Prisoner's Defence. The cap was taken off his head, and thrown to me - I took it up, and ran off with it.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-272]
780. JOSEPH HART was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 1 pig-trough, value 2s., the goods of John Woodcock.
PHILIP JONES. I am a Policeman. I was on duty on the 12th of February, near the Three Colts, Grove-street, Hackney - I saw the prisoner with a pig-trough under his arm; I asked where he got it - he said he bought it of a baker, for 2s., on the Saturday night - I said I was not altogether satisfied; and in taking him to the station, he said it was of no use telling me lies, that he found it in the field.
JOHN WOODCOCK. I live in Wick-lane, Hackney, and am a market-gardener. This trough is mine; I can swear to it by the two ends - I had seen it safe about five o'clock on the Saturday evening, and it was taken before seven the next morning - the prisoner was taken about one hundred and fifty yards from my house.
Prisoner's Defence. I honestly picked it up in the field, about half-past six o'clock in the morning - it was laying down by a bit of cow dung.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Seven Years.

First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
781. WILLIAM SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of February, 1 glass goblet, value 1s., and 2 wineglasses, value 1s., the goods of James Soane.
BENJAMIN WILLMERE. I live with James Soane, a chinaman, in Oxford-street - these glasses were in a basket, outside the door; the prisoner was brought into the shop in the evening; I saw them found on him - I knew them to be master's.
GEORGE STRATTON. I am a butcher, and live next door to Soane. I saw the prisoner take a glass from the basket, and walk away - I followed, and gave him in charge, I saw two wine glasses and a goblet found on him.
JOSIAH EVANS. I am a Policeman. I found the glasses in the prisoner's hat.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Seven Days.
[trialAccount t18320216-274]
782. ALFRED WARREN was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 dead fowl, value 2s., the goods of George Brooks.
MARTHA BROOKS. I am the wife of George Brooks, a poulterer, of Hill-street, Clapton. On the 16th of February, about seven o'clock, I missed three fowls from the shop-window; Williams brought the prisoner to the shop, with a fowl, which I am certain of - I had plucked it, and noticed it particularly.
HENRY WILLIAMS. I am a Policeman. About twenty minutes past seven o'clock in the evening, I took the prisoner in Kingsland, with another man - the prisoner had a fowl under his jacket; he said he had bought it of a countryman, at Tottenham, for 1s. - his companion made off; I took the fowl to Brooks' next morning, and she claimed it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 15. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18320216-275]
783. SARAH JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 1 purse, value 2d., and 4 sovereigns, the property of George Arundel, from his person.
GEORGE ARUNDEL. I live at Chelsea, and am not in business. On Friday night last, about eleven o'clock, I was in Drury-lane, with Mr. Pinnel, and saw the prisoner; I think she spoke to me first - we went into a public-house, in Drury-lane with her; I think it is the White Horse - two girls went into the house, but she was alone; I was sober - I had been dining with a friend; we staid there a very few minutes - she sat next to me; we had something to drink - Pinnel paid for it; I then observed on the seat a paper or two, which had come from my pocket - I felt, and my purse was gone; I am quite sure it was safe when I went to the house - the other girls sat on the other side of me - we were in front of the bar; I told Pinnel I had lost my purse, and suspected the prisoner; he took hold of her hand - I went out for an officer, but before I returned they had got one; I found my purse in the officer's hands.
Cross-examined by MR. WALESBY. Q. Where had you dined? A. With a friend, near Clerkenwell - I recollect seeing a band of musicians in Red Lion-passage; I stood listening to them - I am fond of music, and very likely asked them to play particular tunes; I did not ask them to follow me into Holborn - I did not dance to the tunes; I will not swear I did not beat time with my foot - I am generally merry; I did not caper nor dance - I saw the prisoner there, and she remained in our company all the way to Drury-lane; there were two other women with the music, who, I believe, were in our company before I saw the prisoner - we met them in Red Lion-passage; we all five went into a public-house in Drury-lane - I do not know what I drank there; I do not think the prisoner was affected with liquor - certainly not so as not to know what she said or did - I told the officer there were six or seven sovereigns in my purse; I made a mistake, there were but four - I recollected having paid away two and changed one.
RICHARD PINNEL. I live in Long-acre, with my mother - I am out of business. Mr. Arundel and I dined together, between two and three o'clock, and left thehouse about six or seven; we went to Clerkenwell after dinner - we had dined at the York Arms, Shirelane, which I kept two years ago; it is not frequented by bad women, particularly - Mr. Arundel then went to call on a friend of his in Clerkenwell; I waited while he went in - we returned through Red Lion-square, met two or three women there round the music, and took two or three of them into Holborn, to a house kept by Mr. Thomas, stopped there about a quarter of an hour and drank- it is a wine-vaults; we then went to a wine-vaults in Drury-lane with the three women; the prisoner was one of them - they had followed us; Mr. Arundel missed his purse - I held the prisoner's hands, and he went for an officer; the prisoner then told me if I would let her go she would give me the purse, and gave it me from her bosom- it contained four sovereigns.
Cross-examined. Q. You dined at the York Arms? A. Yes - we had wine for dinner; we got to Red Lion-square between eight and nine o'clock - we had stopped at a public-house on the way; I recollect the music in Red Lion-passage - I did not dance, nor did he; Mr. Arundel might move round with a lady - there were several women about, and two ladies were talking to us; the prisoner was there - we all five went into Holborn; I had some sherry and bitters - we treated the girls with what they chose; the prisoner was not intoxicated - I do not think I drank there; I swear the prisoner was not intoxicated; I was perfectly sober - how the purse got into her bosom - I cannot tell.
GEORGE CONNER. I am pot-boy at the White Lion, Drury-lane - I saw the prisoner there with two more bad girls - I saw her take the purse out of her bosom, and give it to Pinnel; she was tipsy, but not insensible.
Cross-examined. Q. Was Arundel rather tipsy? A. No, he was cheerful.
HENRY BURBIDGE. I am a Policeman. I received the prisoner in charge; Pinnel handed me the purse with four sovereigns in it - the prisoner said she was drunk, and was not aware of having put it into her bosom.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you notice whether she was tipsy? A. She appeared rather so; she fainted away two or three times.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in Red Lion-square - the gentleman came to me; the music was playing - he asked me to go and have a glass with him; we went to Holborn- he asked the music to follow; I went into a gin-shop with him, and got very much intoxicated.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320216-276]
NEW COURT. WEDNESDAY, FEBRUARY 22.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
784. GEORGE PARKER was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 1 carriage-strap, value 2s., the goods of John Trotter.
WILLIAM BURNABY. I am footman to John Trotter, Esq., of Park-place, Edgware-road. This strap was taken from the hind part of the carriage, as it was going down Oxford-street, from Covent-garden, at half-past eleven o'clock on the night of the 17th of February; I was inside, and saw the prisoner come from the pavement, get on the step, and take it; I stopped the coach, got out, and took the prisoner with it on the spot: I got into the carriage on purpose, as we had lost so many straps.
JOSEPH STANNAND. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner, and have the strap, which I took out of his waistcoat pocket - it is the check brace.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18320216-277]
785. PETER PERKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of February, 1 silk gown, value 9s., and 2 suits of boy's clothes, the goods of John Garrard.
JOHN GARRARD. I live in Hackney-road, and am a clothes salesman. On the 6th of February, about eight o'clock in the morning, I was opening my shop, and taking the shutters backwards; I was asked by a woman if I had lost any thing - I looked, and said I had: she said a man had come out with something, which appeared to be a silk gown and some boys' clothes, and gone down the next turning; I went, but could not see him.
ESTHER GILHAM. I was coming across the road, and saw a man come out of the shop, with a bundle; he looked round, and the prisoner came out after him - they hurried down Austin-street; the prisoner is one of them - I described him to Vann, and he took him.
THOMAS VANN. I took the prisoner from Gilham's description.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-278]
786. JAMES PHELPS was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of March, 1831, 3 bushels of wheat, value 30s., the goods of George Corfe.
WILLIAM BARRETT. I am an officer of Isleworth and Twickenham. On the 22nd of March, 1831, about two o'clock in the morning, I fell in with the prisoner and another, near Sir Benjamin Hobhouse 's gate, three or four hundred yards from the prosecutor's - it was in the high road to London; the prisoner was first - I laid hold of him, and I saw behind him a man carrying a sack - I put the prisoner aside, and seized the sack; the man who had it struggled with me, and we both went down, but I still held the sack; the prisoner begged me to let it pass - I said they might go for that time, but I would retain the property; they went on - a great deal of the wheat came out of the sack; I took it, as well as I could, to the prosecutor's - the next morning I went to the barn; I saw a little track of wheat from there to the high road, and on to the place where I met the men, where it had come out in a body; I compared it with what was in the barn, it corresponded - the other man has since been transported for another robbery - I did not find the prisoner till last Wednesday night; I knew him before - he had lived in the neighbourhood twelve months.
Prisoner. Q. Can you say I was near the man? A. Yes, you was close to him; I told you I would bring you to justice, but you might go that time.
JOSEPH FULLER. I am a labourer in the service of Mr. Corfe; his barn was locked up, and all was right - I went the next morning at six o'clock, and found the lock had been removed, and somebody had been in the barn - I missed about three bushels of wheat - the sack was not my master's; there was a track of wheat from the barn to the main road - I had threshed the wheat; I have been all my life acquainted with wheat - I have no doubt it was Mr. Corfe's.
GEORGE CORFE. I am a farmer. This wheat was taken from my barn - I compared it with what was left, and have no doubt it was mine; the prisoner had worked for me, and knew my premises.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing of the wheat - I had been watching Mr. Corfe's premises when he had expected to be set on fire; I heard a piece of work that night - I went back, and saw Young and Barrett struggling; Barrett said, "Young, you may go, and if I had known you had been this road I would have gone some other;" I gave Barrett a lift up with the wheat.
GUILTY. Aged 30. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-279]
787. THOMAS WEBSTER was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of February, 1 copper builer, value 10s., the goods of Henry Wood, and fixed to a building of his.
WILLIAM CLARK. I am an officer. On the 13th of February, about a quarter before seven o'clock in the evening, the prisoner past my house in Clerkenwell, about ten minutes' walk from the building where this copper was taken from - he went into a rag-shop, just below where I lived, to sell it; he came out in two or three minutes, and told another boy it was all right - he went back, and went into the shop again; I took the other boy, and sent a Policeman, who took the prisoner; the other boy was discharged.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am a Policeman. I went and took the prisoner coming out the second time, with the copper in his hand.
HENRY WOOD. I live at Shepherd's-bush - this copper was in a building belonging to me; I have seen it fitted, and it fits exactly - I have not seen the premises this eighteen months; it is my house, and the tenants have left it.
GUILTY. Aged 11. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18320216-280]
788. RICHARD JACKSON was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of February, 2 shirts, value 6s., the goods of William Moore.
TWO OTHER COUNTS, stating them to belong to William Atkinson and Henry Holtzenyer.
SARAH MOORE. I am the wife of William Moore, and am a washer-woman - we live at Tottenham. I had these shirts on the common to dry; one of them belongs to Henry Holtzenyer, and the other to William Atkinson -I was not at home when they were taken.
JAMES LANBOURN. I am a gardener, at Tottenham. On the 15th of February, between one and two o'clock, I saw the prisoner running away with the property concealed in his apron; he might be three hundred yards from the prosecutor's house - I went and took him with the shirts; there were two other persons; we took them all, but the other two were discharged; one of them had been watching near the dung-hill, and he struck me on my arm.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Did you tell the same story before the Grand Jury as you have here? A. Yes; they threw out the bill against Dison and Humphries - I am gardener to Mr. James Roe.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The things were laying in the road, and I took them up and put them in my apron.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-281]
789. GEORGE WELLBANK was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of February, 10 yards of printed cotton, value 5s., the goods of Edward Belton.
EDWARD BELTON. I keep a draper's shop in Lisson-grove. On the 8th of February, about half-past four o'clock, I was at home, but did not see this cotton taken.
MICHAEL JOHNSON. I live next door to the prosecutor; I saw the prisoner and another boy for some time opposite my window; I watched them, and saw them run; I then went out; the prisoner had got the printed cotton, and dropped it on my rails; I suspected he had stolen it - I took it up, and he was pursued and taken; the other. boy got away; I am quite sure the prisoner is the person who was running with the cotton and dropped it; he only ran into the next street.
THOMAS FURSEY. I am an officer. I have the property. (Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence (written.) I passed the shop of the prosecutor; whilst doing so, a boy, who had taken a piece of print, threw it on the ground and ran away; a person from the shop ran after him, and I picked up the print, intending to keep it until the person who had gone in pursuit of the thief returned.
GUILTY. Aged 13. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320216-282]
790. PETER WALES was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 2lbs. of mutton, value 1s. 5d., and 3 lbs. of beef, value 1s. 6d., the property of Henry Rogers.
WILLIAM ROGERS. I am brother to Henry Rogers, a butcher. On the 12th of January, in the evening, I was serving in the shop, and missed some meat off the board, but did not see it taken; the prisoner was taken by the Policeman, who brought the meat back to the shop; he had got half a mile; he had not bought any thing.
THOMAS KELLY. I am a Policeman. On the 12th of January I was in Brick-lane, Old-street; I saw the prisoner and another boy; the prisoner had a basket - I followed them to Golden-lane, and took the basket from the prisoner; I saw two joints of meat in it - I said,"Where did you get this?" he said a boy gave it him; the other boy ran away - I inquired, and found the prosecutor.
WILLIAM ROGERS. I can swear to the meat, by the score of the mutton, and the beef I had cut myself - it was between seven and eight o'clock in the evening.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18320216-283]
791. JAMES WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of February, 1 carpet, value 10s., the goods of Mary Price.
MARY PRICE. I live in Union-walk, Union-street, Kingsland-road; the prisoner lodged eight months with me, and was a shoe-maker; I keep a small clothes-shop. On the 10th of February I missed a piece of carpet - he was not at home then, but came home at night; I sent for an officer, and charged him with taking it - he denied all knowledge of it, but in going to the station he told me it was at the pawnbroker's.
WILLIAM EVETT. I am a pawnbroker I took in this carpet from the prisoner.
-. I am a Police-officer. I took the prisoner; he told us where the carpet was, and said he had taken it. (Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. It was my intention to have redeemed the carpet, or I should have absconded.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Three Months.
